- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### The Geometry of LLM Quantization: GPTQ as Babai's Nearest Plane Algorithm
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18553v1)
- **Authors**: Jiale Chen, Torsten Hoefler, Dan Alistarh
- **Abstract**: Quantizing the weights of large language models (LLMs) from 16-bit to lower bitwidth is the de facto approach to deploy massive transformers onto more affordable accelerators. GPTQ emerged as one of the standard methods for one-shot post-training quantization at LLM scale. Yet, its inner workings are described as a sequence of ad-hoc algebraic updates that obscure any geometric meaning or worst-case guarantees. In this work, we show that, when executed back-to-front (from the last to first dimension) for a linear layer, GPTQ is mathematically identical to Babai's nearest plane algorithm for the classical closest vector problem (CVP) on a lattice defined by the Hessian matrix of the layer's inputs. This equivalence is based on a sophisticated mathematical argument, and has two analytical consequences: (i) the GPTQ error propagation step gains an intuitive geometric interpretation; (ii) GPTQ inherits the error upper bound of Babai's algorithm under the no-clipping condition. Taken together, these results place GPTQ on firm theoretical footing and open the door to importing decades of progress in lattice algorithms towards the design of future quantization algorithms for billion-parameter models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "The Geometry of LLM Quantization: GPTQ as Babai's Nearest Plane Algorithm" establishes a mathematical equivalence between GPTQ (a popular post-training quantization technique for Large Language Models) and Babai's nearest plane algorithm for solving the closest vector problem (CVP) in lattice theory. Specifically, when GPTQ is executed back-to-front, it performs the same operations as Babai's algorithm with a lattice defined by the Hessian matrix of the layer's inputs. This equivalence allows the authors to provide a geometric interpretation of GPTQ's error propagation step and to inherit Babai's error bound, offering a formal guarantee for layer-wise quantization error under certain conditions.  The paper also proposes a new "min-pivot" quantization ordering based on minimizing the trace of the LDL decomposition of the Hessian matrix, potentially improving the quantization process.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in connecting GPTQ, a rather heuristically justified algorithm, to a well-established algorithm from lattice theory. This connection is not immediately obvious from the algebraic definition of GPTQ, and revealing this hidden structure represents a significant contribution. The geometric interpretation of error propagation is also novel and provides a more intuitive understanding of GPTQ's workings. The proposed "min-pivot" ordering strategy offers a practical improvement based on the theoretical connection. While the CVP framework is established, the application and insights derived are definitely non-trivial.

**Significance:** This paper has significant implications for the field of LLM quantization.

*   **Theoretical Foundation:** It provides a firm theoretical foundation for GPTQ, moving beyond the ad-hoc explanation based on algebraic operations. This allows researchers to understand *why* GPTQ works so well, and *when* it might fail.
*   **Error Bounds:**  The inherited error bounds offer a formal guarantee for GPTQ's performance, albeit under the no-clipping condition. This is valuable for assessing the reliability of quantization.
*   **Algorithm Design:** By framing quantization as a CVP problem, the paper opens the door to importing techniques from lattice algorithms (like basis reduction) to improve quantization strategies. This suggests a pathway for designing more efficient and accurate quantization algorithms.
*   **Practical Implications:** The 'min-pivot' ordering strategy has immediate practical implications, potentially improving quantization results with minimal computational overhead.

**Strengths:**

*   **Rigorous Mathematical Analysis:** The paper provides a detailed and rigorous mathematical proof of the equivalence between GPTQ and Babai's algorithm.
*   **Clear Geometric Interpretation:** It offers a clear and intuitive geometric interpretation of GPTQ's error propagation step.
*   **Practical Implications:** The paper's findings have the potential to improve the design of future quantization algorithms and to provide formal guarantees for their performance.
*   **Strong Connections:** It establishes a connection between two disparate fields, potentially stimulating cross-fertilization of ideas.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **No Clipping Constraint:** The inherited error bound is only valid under the no-clipping condition (Z† = Z).  Since clipping is often used in practice, it limits the general applicability of this result. The practicality of this bound needs to be further substantiated.
*   **Basis Reduction Limitation:**  The scale-sensitivity of LLL and incompatibility with clipping make it hard to apply full basis reduction algorithms.
*   **Min-Pivot Ordering:** The "min-pivot" ordering strategy has not been extensively validated. More empirical testing is needed to assess its effectiveness in practice, though it has a geometric plausibility. The trace minimization for pivot selection is a common practice, and is thus not groundbreaking.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper successfully bridges two distinct fields, offers non-trivial insights into an established algorithm, and opens a new avenue for LLM compression research. While the no-clipping condition and early-stage validation of the "min-pivot" strategy limit the current direct applicability of results, the foundational impact is significant.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### HARLF: Hierarchical Reinforcement Learning and Lightweight LLM-Driven Sentiment Integration for Financial Portfolio Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18560v1)
- **Authors**: Benjamin Coriat, Eric Benhamou
- **Abstract**: This paper presents a novel hierarchical framework for portfolio optimization, integrating lightweight Large Language Models (LLMs) with Deep Reinforcement Learning (DRL) to combine sentiment signals from financial news with traditional market indicators. Our three-tier architecture employs base RL agents to process hybrid data, meta-agents to aggregate their decisions, and a super-agent to merge decisions based on market data and sentiment analysis. Evaluated on data from 2018 to 2024, after training on 2000-2017, the framework achieves a 26% annualized return and a Sharpe ratio of 1.2, outperforming equal-weighted and S&P 500 benchmarks. Key contributions include scalable cross-modal integration, a hierarchical RL structure for enhanced stability, and open-source reproducibility.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper.

**Summary:**

The paper "HARLF: Hierarchical Reinforcement Learning and Lightweight LLM-Driven Sentiment Integration for Financial Portfolio Optimization" proposes a novel hierarchical reinforcement learning (HRL) framework for financial portfolio optimization. The framework integrates sentiment analysis from financial news using lightweight Large Language Models (LLMs) like FinBERT with traditional market indicators. The system employs a three-tier architecture consisting of base RL agents processing hybrid data (financial metrics and sentiment scores), meta-agents aggregating decisions from the base agents, and a super-agent synthesizing cross-modal signals to generate final portfolio allocations. The results, obtained from training the model from 2003 to 2017 and testing it from 2018 to 2024, demonstrate superior performance against equal-weighted and S&P 500 benchmarks, with a 26% annualized return and a Sharpe ratio of 1.2. The key contributions are cross-modal integration, hierarchical aggregation, and practical applicability. The paper discusses the architecture, data pipelines, RL agents, portfolio constraints, and benchmarks in detail, and provides open-access code for reproducibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a hierarchical RL architecture for portfolio optimization, which is a significant contribution. Combining LLMs for sentiment analysis with DRL in a hierarchical manner to improve performance is not very common and provides a more structured and potentially more interpretable approach than flat RL models.
*   **Cross-modal Integration:** The seamless integration of LLM-derived sentiment scores with structured financial data is a strong point. This allows the agent to leverage both qualitative and quantitative information.
*   **Hierarchical Structure:** The three-layer architecture is well-motivated and explained. It provides a way to decompose the problem into manageable sub-problems, potentially improving stability and scalability. The ablation study to identify the contributions of each tier would add to its significance.
*   **Practical Applicability:** The use of a lightweight LLM (FinBERT) enhances practical deployment in latency-sensitive and transparency-critical applications.
*   **Reproducibility:** The authors have made their code publicly available, enabling other researchers to reproduce and build upon their work. This is essential for scientific advancement.
*   **Demonstrated Performance:** The reported results (26% annualized return, Sharpe ratio of 1.2) are impressive and suggest that the framework is effective. The detailed benchmark comparison with existing RL strategies add to the credibility of the obtained results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty in Individual Components:** While the architecture is novel, the individual components (FinBERT, DDPG, PPO, SAC) are well-established. The real novelty lies in the specific *combination* and hierarchical structure, but this needs to be further highlighted.
*   **Market Realism:** Although the strict rules imposed in the experements mimic realistic investment scenarios, it is important to note the transaction costs are excluded.
*   **Limited Exploration of LLM Impact:** The paper uses FinBERT, but it could explore more deeply how different LLMs (larger or fine-tuned on different datasets) affect performance. A comparison with other models could show to what extent the improvements come from the overall framework and not simply from the sentiment data itself.
*   **Synchronous Data Assumption:** As the authors acknowledge, the assumption of synchronously available data inputs is a limitation for real-world applications. The impact of this limitation should be discussed more thoroughly.
*   **Lack of Ablation Study:** The paper would be significantly strengthened by including an ablation study. This would help to quantify the contribution of each component (e.g., base agents, meta-agents, sentiment data) to the overall performance. For example, what is the impact if the hierarchy is not applied and we simply use all base agents.
*   **Robustness to Extreme Events:** The paper does not explicitly address the robustness of the framework to extreme market events or adversarial attacks.
*   **Overfitting Potential:** Highlighting mitigation strategies to avoid overfitting.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance lies in providing a practical and effective framework for incorporating sentiment analysis into portfolio optimization. The hierarchical approach offers a potential path towards more interpretable and scalable RL-based trading systems. However, the limitations regarding real-world data asynchronicity, robustness, and further LLM exploration somewhat limit its immediate impact. The open-source code will allow others to validate and extend the research.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7.0**. The paper presents a reasonably novel and potentially impactful contribution to the field of financial portfolio optimization through its hierarchical RL framework and the integration of sentiment analysis. The demonstrated performance is encouraging, and the open-source code is a valuable asset. However, the limitations discussed above, especially the need for an ablation study to clarify the contributions of individual components and explore more comprehensively the role of LLMs, and the limitations relating to transaction costs and data synchronocity slightly diminish the paper's current significance. Future work that addresses these limitations could significantly enhance the value of this research.

Score: 7.0

- **Classification**: q-fin.PM
- **Score**: 7/10

### Adversarial Distribution Matching for Diffusion Distillation Towards Efficient Image and Video Synthesis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18569v1)
- **Authors**: Yanzuo Lu, Yuxi Ren, Xin Xia, Shanchuan Lin, Xing Wang, Xuefeng Xiao, Andy J. Ma, Xiaohua Xie, Jian-Huang Lai
- **Abstract**: Distribution Matching Distillation (DMD) is a promising score distillation technique that compresses pre-trained teacher diffusion models into efficient one-step or multi-step student generators. Nevertheless, its reliance on the reverse Kullback-Leibler (KL) divergence minimization potentially induces mode collapse (or mode-seeking) in certain applications. To circumvent this inherent drawback, we propose Adversarial Distribution Matching (ADM), a novel framework that leverages diffusion-based discriminators to align the latent predictions between real and fake score estimators for score distillation in an adversarial manner. In the context of extremely challenging one-step distillation, we further improve the pre-trained generator by adversarial distillation with hybrid discriminators in both latent and pixel spaces. Different from the mean squared error used in DMD2 pre-training, our method incorporates the distributional loss on ODE pairs collected from the teacher model, and thus providing a better initialization for score distillation fine-tuning in the next stage. By combining the adversarial distillation pre-training with ADM fine-tuning into a unified pipeline termed DMDX, our proposed method achieves superior one-step performance on SDXL compared to DMD2 while consuming less GPU time. Additional experiments that apply multi-step ADM distillation on SD3-Medium, SD3.5-Large, and CogVideoX set a new benchmark towards efficient image and video synthesis.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Adversarial Distribution Matching (ADM), a novel framework for distilling diffusion models into efficient image and video generators. Addressing the mode collapse issue inherent in Distribution Matching Distillation (DMD), ADM leverages diffusion-based discriminators to align the latent predictions of real and fake score estimators in an adversarial manner.  For one-step distillation, the paper further refines the pre-trained generator using adversarial distillation with hybrid discriminators in both latent and pixel spaces. This includes incorporating a distributional loss on ODE pairs gathered from the teacher model, resulting in better initialization. The combined approach, named DMDX, outperforms DMD2 in one-step SDXL generation with less GPU time.  The paper also demonstrates the effectiveness of multi-step ADM distillation on SD3-Medium, SD3.5-Large, and CogVideoX, establishing new benchmarks for efficient image and video synthesis.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the adversarial approach to distribution matching for score distillation. Instead of using predefined divergence metrics (like KL divergence which is susceptible to mode collapse), the paper proposes learning a data-driven discrepancy measure using a discriminator.  The hybrid discriminator (latent + pixel space) pre-training strategy also offers a contribution, particularly for stabilizing the difficult one-step distillation. It draws inspiration from existing work (SDXL-Lightning, Rectified Flow, LADD) but combines and adapts them for the specific problem of stabilizing score distillation and circumventing limitations in divergence based approaches.

* **Significance:**  The significance lies in the improved efficiency and fidelity of distilled diffusion models, especially for one-step generation.  Achieving comparable or superior results to existing methods with a significant reduction in NFE has a direct impact on the practical usability of these models. Setting new benchmarks on well-known datasets demonstrates the tangible improvement. The emphasis on video generation distillation adds to its significance.

* **Strengths:**
    * Addresses a key limitation of existing score distillation techniques (mode collapse).
    * The adversarial training approach provides a more adaptive way of matching distributions.
    * The hybrid discriminator and ODE-pair based pre-training enhance stability and initialization.
    * Demonstrated performance gains on multiple image and video synthesis datasets.
    * Clear explanation of the method and its relation to existing techniques.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * While the paper shows empirically improved performance, the theoretical justification for *why* the learned discrepancy measure of the discriminator is superior to predefined divergence metrics could be stronger. A more in-depth theoretical analysis of the adversarial loss would enhance the paper.  Although the authors perform limited exploration of the effect of using higher order ODE solvers to reduce the number of "diffusion steps" with respect to sampling quality, this may have been a worthwhile area for further exploration
    * The approach relies on several components and hyperparameters (discriminator architectures, weighting factors), adding complexity to the training process.  A more thorough sensitivity analysis could be helpful. The implementation details given are rather high level and do not describe the specifics of neural network architectures or parameterizations employed
    * The reliance on pre-trained diffusion models as part of the discriminator makes the method somewhat dependent on the quality of these existing models, a fact somewhat glossed over by the author

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a more robust and efficient approach to diffusion model distillation. The adversarial distribution matching technique could be adopted in other applications where distribution alignment is crucial. The improvements in video generation could accelerate research in that area. It also presents a strong motivation for further exploration of learned discrepancy measures in generative modeling.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a well-motivated and empirically validated approach to improve diffusion model distillation. The adversarial distribution matching technique offers a significant improvement over existing methods, particularly in stabilizing training and addressing mode collapse. While a deeper theoretical justification and further analysis of the impact of key hyperparameters and dependence on the pre-trained models would strengthen the paper, the demonstrated performance gains and the potential for wider adoption justify a score of 7.5.  The novelty lies in the *combination* of techniques and their specific application to score distillation, more than a completely groundbreaking theoretical insight. The weaknesses are more about further exploration and deeper theoretical grounding, not fundamental flaws in the approach itself.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Wide-In, Narrow-Out: Revokable Decoding for Efficient and Effective DLLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18578v1)
- **Authors**: Feng Hong, Geng Yu, Yushi Ye, Haicheng Huang, Huangjie Zheng, Ya Zhang, Yanfeng Wang, Jiangchao Yao
- **Abstract**: Diffusion Large Language Models (DLLMs) have emerged as a compelling alternative to Autoregressive models, designed for fast parallel generation. However, existing DLLMs are plagued by a severe quality-speed trade-off, where faster parallel decoding leads to significant performance degradation. We attribute this to the irreversibility of standard decoding in DLLMs, which is easily polarized into the wrong decoding direction along with early error context accumulation. To resolve this, we introduce Wide-In, Narrow-Out (WINO), a training-free decoding algorithm that enables revokable decoding in DLLMs. WINO employs a parallel draft-and-verify mechanism, aggressively drafting multiple tokens while simultaneously using the model's bidirectional context to verify and re-mask suspicious ones for refinement. Verified in open-source DLLMs like LLaDA and MMaDA, WINO is shown to decisively improve the quality-speed trade-off. For instance, on the GSM8K math benchmark, it accelerates inference by 6$\times$ while improving accuracy by 2.58%; on Flickr30K captioning, it achieves a 10$\times$ speedup with higher performance. More comprehensive experiments are conducted to demonstrate the superiority and provide an in-depth understanding of WINO.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces WINO (Wide-In, Narrow-Out), a novel, training-free decoding algorithm designed to improve the quality-speed trade-off in Diffusion Large Language Models (DLLMs). WINO tackles the issue of error propagation in parallel decoding by enabling revokable decoding. It works by employing a parallel draft-and-verify mechanism. The "Wide-In" component aggressively drafts multiple tokens in parallel. The "Narrow-Out" component uses the model's bidirectional context to verify the drafted tokens and re-mask suspicious ones for refinement.  Experiments on open-source DLLMs like LLaDA and MMaDA demonstrate that WINO improves inference speed and accuracy across various language and visual-language tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of introducing revokable decoding to DLLMs is novel. The standard DLLM decoding process commits to tokens generated early in the process, which can lead to error accumulation and hinder parallel decoding. The draft-and-verify mechanism offers a way to correct errors introduced in early stages by using subsequent more context-aware stages. The combination of "wide-in" with aggressive token generation and "narrow-out" with a verification step is a unique and potentially valuable contribution. The training-free nature is also an important benefit.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper lies in its ability to address the speed-quality trade-off that has plagued open-source DLLMs, preventing them from becoming a viable alternative to autoregressive models. By enabling faster, more accurate decoding, the proposed method opens the door for more practical applications of DLLMs. The performance gains reported across a range of benchmarks, including GSM8K and Flickr30K, further highlight the importance of the approach. The consistent improvements over LLaDA and MMaDA shows that WINO is model agnostic.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly identifies and articulates the problem of the quality-speed trade-off in DLLMs due to the irreversibility of standard decoding.
    *   **Well-Defined Method:** The WINO algorithm is clearly explained, with a logical justification for its design choices. The draft-and-verify process is easy to understand.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experiments are extensive, covering a range of tasks and models, which strengthens the findings and demonstrates the general applicability of WINO.
    *   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study on the verification module effectively demonstrates the importance of this key component in maintaining performance.
    *   **Analysis of Decoding Dynamics:** The case study providing insight into the improvements through iterative error-correction is valuable.
    *   **Training-Free:** WINO is a training-free method, and thereby can be easily applied to current models.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The paper mentions tuning the drafting threshold, but the selection process or sensitivity of performance to hyperparameter choice for the verification threshold also could be more explored.
    *   **Limited Scope of Base Models:** While the evaluations use existing open-source models LLaDA and MMaDA, these are still under active development, and may not represent the best potential of DLLMs. Performance on other popular models would be helpful.
    *   **Semi-autoregressive limitation:** The method is mostly limited to semi-autoregressive generation, that may not extract the full potential of DLLMs. A full-diffusion decoder could give further performance improvements.

*   **Potential Impact:** The work has the potential to significantly influence the design and adoption of DLLMs. By providing a simple yet effective decoding algorithm, the work can make DLLMs more competitive with autoregressive models in terms of both speed and accuracy. The training-free nature could accelerate the adoption of the method within the community.
The analysis of memory cost is helpful in estimating the increased resource costs and should be reported in other implementations as well.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the novelty of the approach, the thorough experimental validation, and the potential impact on the field of DLLMs, I assign the following score:

Score: 7

This score reflects a well-designed, well-executed, and significant contribution. While the paper's current scope is somewhat limited by the specific DLLM architectures used and the focus on semi-autoregressive decoding, the core ideas are novel and have the potential to inspire further research. The comprehensive experimental results lend strong support to the paper's claims, and the training-free aspect increases the practicality and immediate applicability of WINO. Future work could involve exploring the method's applicability to other models, a deeper theoretical understanding of the framework, a more extensive hyperparameter evaluation or a combination with caching methods.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### DR.EHR: Dense Retrieval for Electronic Health Record with Knowledge Injection and Synthetic Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18583v1)
- **Authors**: Zhengyun Zhao, Huaiyuan Ying, Yue Zhong, Sheng Yu
- **Abstract**: Electronic Health Records (EHRs) are pivotal in clinical practices, yet their retrieval remains a challenge mainly due to semantic gap issues. Recent advancements in dense retrieval offer promising solutions but existing models, both general-domain and biomedical-domain, fall short due to insufficient medical knowledge or mismatched training corpora. This paper introduces \texttt{DR.EHR}, a series of dense retrieval models specifically tailored for EHR retrieval. We propose a two-stage training pipeline utilizing MIMIC-IV discharge summaries to address the need for extensive medical knowledge and large-scale training data. The first stage involves medical entity extraction and knowledge injection from a biomedical knowledge graph, while the second stage employs large language models to generate diverse training data. We train two variants of \texttt{DR.EHR}, with 110M and 7B parameters, respectively. Evaluated on the CliniQ benchmark, our models significantly outperforms all existing dense retrievers, achieving state-of-the-art results. Detailed analyses confirm our models' superiority across various match and query types, particularly in challenging semantic matches like implication and abbreviation. Ablation studies validate the effectiveness of each pipeline component, and supplementary experiments on EHR QA datasets demonstrate the models' generalizability on natural language questions, including complex ones with multiple entities. This work significantly advances EHR retrieval, offering a robust solution for clinical applications.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "DR.EHR: Dense Retrieval for Electronic Health Record with Knowledge Injection and Synthetic Data."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DR.EHR, a dense retrieval model specifically designed for electronic health record (EHR) retrieval.  The authors address the limitations of existing general-domain and biomedical dense retrieval models, which often lack sufficient medical knowledge or are trained on mismatched corpora, by proposing a two-stage training pipeline. The first stage involves knowledge injection from a biomedical knowledge graph (BIOS) using medical entity extraction, and abbreviation expansion. The second stage leverages large language models (LLMs) to generate synthetic training data to improve the diversity of the training examples. The model is trained using contrastive learning with in-batch negatives.  The authors evaluate DR.EHR on the CliniQ benchmark and demonstrate state-of-the-art performance, particularly on challenging semantic matching types.  They also conduct ablation studies to validate the effectiveness of each component in the training pipeline and showcase generalizability on EHR question answering datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its specific application of dense retrieval techniques to the EHR domain with a customized training pipeline. While the individual components (knowledge injection, synthetic data generation) are not entirely new, their combination and adaptation within the context of EHR retrieval is a significant contribution. The careful selection of BIOS and the specific prompting strategies for LLM-based data synthesis represent a well-engineered approach. The focus on overcoming the semantic gap issue in EHR retrieval, particularly in challenging scenarios such as abbreviations and implicit inference is also laudable.
*   **Significance:** The work addresses a critical problem in clinical practice: efficient EHR retrieval. Improved EHR retrieval systems can directly benefit physicians by enabling faster access to relevant patient information, leading to better and potentially faster diagnosis and treatment. Demonstrating superior performance on a public benchmark like CliniQ establishes a new state-of-the-art and provides a strong baseline for future research. The insights gained from the ablation studies regarding the contribution of each component provide valuable guidance for other researchers in this area. The extension of the model's evaluation to EHR QA datasets, even as a supplementary analysis, suggests its broader applicability and generalizability.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong empirical results:** The paper presents compelling empirical evidence of DR.EHR's superiority over existing models on the CliniQ benchmark.
    *   **Well-designed training pipeline:** The two-stage training pipeline is thoughtfully designed to address the specific challenges of EHR retrieval, effectively leveraging knowledge injection and synthetic data generation.
    *   **Detailed analysis:** The authors conduct thorough analyses to understand the model's strengths and weaknesses, including semantic match assessment and query type assessment.
    *   **Ablation studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component of the training pipeline.
    *   **Generalizability demonstration:** The supplementary experiments on EHR QA datasets demonstrate the model's generalizability to natural language questions.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on LLMs:** The synthetic data generation relies heavily on LLMs, which can introduce biases or inaccuracies. While the authors perform manual validation, a more rigorous analysis of the generated data quality would be beneficial.
    *   **Limited benchmark coverage:** The primary evaluation is conducted on CliniQ, which, while useful, may not fully represent the diversity of real-world EHR retrieval scenarios. More evaluation on other diverse EHR datasets would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Complexity:** The pipeline involves multiple steps and components (knowledge injection, LLM-based data generation), which might make it harder to reproduce or adapt to different settings compared to simpler models. More detail on the specific choices made in the pipeline would be valuable for readers.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a significant influence on the field of EHR retrieval. The proposed approach provides a robust and effective solution to address the challenges of semantic gap and insufficient training data. The release of DR.EHR and the detailed analysis in the paper will serve as a valuable resource for other researchers in this area. The paper encourages further research on leveraging knowledge graphs and synthetic data for improving EHR retrieval models.

**Score:** 8

**Justification:**

I assign a score of 8 because the paper makes a substantial contribution to the field by presenting a novel and effective dense retrieval model specifically tailored for EHR retrieval. The strong empirical results on a public benchmark, the detailed analyses, and the ablation studies provide compelling evidence of the model's superiority. While there are some limitations, such as the dependence on LLMs and limited benchmark coverage, these do not significantly detract from the overall quality and significance of the work. The carefully crafted pipeline and the promising generalizability results suggest this is an important step towards making EHR retrieval more practical for clinical application.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 8/10

### AQuilt: Weaving Logic and Self-Inspection into Low-Cost, High-Relevance Data Synthesis for Specialist LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18584v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaopeng Ke, Hexuan Deng, Xuebo Liu, Jun Rao, Zhenxi Song, Jun Yu, Min Zhang
- **Abstract**: Despite the impressive performance of large language models (LLMs) in general domains, they often underperform in specialized domains. Existing approaches typically rely on data synthesis methods and yield promising results by using unlabeled data to capture domain-specific features. However, these methods either incur high computational costs or suffer from performance limitations, while also demonstrating insufficient generalization across different tasks. To address these challenges, we propose AQuilt, a framework for constructing instruction-tuning data for any specialized domains from corresponding unlabeled data, including Answer, Question, Unlabeled data, Inspection, Logic, and Task type. By incorporating logic and inspection, we encourage reasoning processes and self-inspection to enhance model performance. Moreover, customizable task instructions enable high-quality data generation for any task. As a result, we construct a dataset of 703k examples to train a powerful data synthesis model. Experiments show that AQuilt is comparable to DeepSeek-V3 while utilizing just 17% of the production cost. Further analysis demonstrates that our generated data exhibits higher relevance to downstream tasks. Source code, models, and scripts are available at https://github.com/Krueske/AQuilt.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the AQuilt paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AQuilt, a novel framework for generating high-quality, domain-specific instruction-tuning data for specialized Large Language Models (LLMs) from unlabeled data. AQuilt integrates Answer, Question, Unlabeled data, Inspection, Logic, and Task type to synthesize training data. The key innovations are: (1) using a low-cost data synthesis model instead of relying on expensive commercial LLMs; (2) incorporating "Logic" and "Inspection" mechanisms to enhance reasoning and self-assessment during data generation; and (3) using customizable task instructions for versatile task coverage. Experiments demonstrate that AQuilt can achieve performance comparable to DeepSeek-V3 at a fraction of the cost, suggesting improved efficiency and data quality.  The paper also highlights the importance of relevance-aware data filtering and shows improvements from the logic and self-inspection components. The authors provide access to code, models and data, increasing reproducibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty stems from the integration of several key ideas: low-cost data synthesis with logic and self-inspection, coupled with a task-type aware training regime.  While individual components (data synthesis from unlabeled data, logic-based reasoning) are not entirely novel, the combination and targeted optimization for specialized domains present a valuable contribution. The core contribution is weaving them into an overall framework (AQuilt). The incorporation of both logic and self-inspection in a single framework for data synthesis is a strong aspect of the paper.
*   **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing the critical challenge of adapting LLMs to specialized domains, where high-quality training data is often scarce and expensive to produce. AQuilt offers a more accessible and cost-effective approach to data synthesis, making it potentially attractive to researchers and practitioners working with specialized LLMs. The demonstrated performance at a much lower cost is a compelling argument for AQuilt's practical value. The analysis of the impact of logic and self-inspection offers insights valuable for future research in this area.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Cost-effectiveness:** Demonstrates a significant reduction in computational cost compared to using large commercial LLMs for data synthesis.
    *   **Data quality:** Introduces logic and inspection mechanisms to improve the quality and relevance of the generated data.
    *   **Task generalization:** Expands the Task type to facilitate generalization to unseen tasks during training.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** Includes ablation studies to isolate the effects of key components and domain relevance analysis to back up claims.
    *   **Reproducibility:** Publicly releases code, models, and data, enhancing reproducibility and facilitating further research.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on DeepSeek-V3 (initially):** The initial process relies on DeepSeek-V3 for distillation, which is then used to train a more compact model. This raises the question: how well will AQuilt performs without a large distillation source.
    *   **Limited Domain Diversity:** While the paper covers multiple tasks, it could benefit from demonstrating AQuilt's effectiveness across a wider range of *specialized* domains (e.g., finance, manufacturing, robotics). The CEVAL tasks focus on high school / middle school subjects. A more compelling experiment would include a legal training.
    *   **Evaluation Focus:** While the component contributions are thoroughly tested, the integration is still a distillation from Deepseek V3. The method improves the "downstream" effect on a target model, rather than outperforming V3. This puts a ceiling on the novelty. The performance is comparable to the best results using DeepSeek-V3. A full comparison of quality to large, un-distilled models might be stronger.

*   **Potential Influence:** AQuilt has the potential to influence future research in:

    *   Cost-effective data synthesis techniques for specialized LLMs.
    *   Integration of reasoning and self-assessment mechanisms in data generation pipelines.
    *   Task-agnostic instruction-tuning approaches for improved generalization.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:** AQuilt presents a novel and practically significant approach to domain-specific data synthesis. The strengths, including cost-effectiveness, data quality improvements, and task generalization capabilities, outweigh the identified weaknesses. However, the dependence on large models for initial data synthesis, the somewhat limited domain diversity in the evaluation, and not clearly outperforming DeepSeek V3, prevent a higher score.  The integration of the ideas is well-executed and the thorough evaluation demonstrates its effectiveness. The significance lies in the cost reduction and potential impact on specialized LLM development. While it does not represent a fundamental breakthrough, AQuilt is a solid contribution with promising potential for further development and adoption.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Linear Memory SE(2) Invariant Attention
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18597v1)
- **Authors**: Ethan Pronovost, Neha Boloor, Peter Schleede, Noureldin Hendy, Andres Morales, Nicholas Roy
- **Abstract**: Processing spatial data is a key component in many learning tasks for autonomous driving such as motion forecasting, multi-agent simulation, and planning. Prior works have demonstrated the value in using SE(2) invariant network architectures that consider only the relative poses between objects (e.g. other agents, scene features such as traffic lanes). However, these methods compute the relative poses for all pairs of objects explicitly, requiring quadratic memory. In this work, we propose a mechanism for SE(2) invariant scaled dot-product attention that requires linear memory relative to the number of objects in the scene. Our SE(2) invariant transformer architecture enjoys the same scaling properties that have benefited large language models in recent years. We demonstrate experimentally that our approach is practical to implement and improves performance compared to comparable non-invariant architectures.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel method for SE(2) invariant attention that scales linearly with the number of objects in a scene, addressing the quadratic memory requirements of existing approaches. The method, named SE(2) Fourier, leverages a Fourier series approximation to encode relative positions in a block-diagonal matrix of 2D rotations, enabling the use of standard scaled dot-product attention (SDPA) with linear memory consumption. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of their approach in agent simulation, showing performance improvements over comparable non-invariant architectures and existing RoPE methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using a Fourier series approximation for SE(2) invariant attention to achieve linear memory scaling is novel.  While RoPE and its extensions address relative position encoding, they are either limited to abelian groups or, like GTA, do not effectively encode relative *pose* in SE(2) with linear memory. SE(2) Fourier directly addresses the challenge of quadratic memory in SE(2) invariant architectures.

*   **Significance:**  The significance lies in its potential impact on large-scale multi-agent systems.  The quadratic memory bottleneck has hindered the application of SE(2) invariant networks to scenarios with a large number of interacting agents. By achieving linear scaling, the proposed method opens the door to training more sophisticated models with larger contexts. Agent simulation is an important area for robotics and autonomous driving, and this efficiency improvement is important.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Mathematical Rigor:** The paper provides a clear mathematical derivation of the Fourier approximation and demonstrates its equivalence to the standard scaled dot-product attention.

    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experimental results on agent simulation show performance gains compared to non-invariant and existing SE(2) approaches, particularly in scenarios involving turning trajectories.  This validates the practical utility of the proposed method.

    *   **Practical Implementation:** By enabling standard SDPА and Flash Attention, the approach can easily be adopted and benefit from advances in attention mechanisms.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Approximation Error:** The Fourier series approximation introduces error, which is directly related to the key position magnitude and the number of basis elements. While the paper demonstrates that this error can be kept within acceptable bounds, a more in-depth analysis of the trade-off between accuracy and computational cost would be beneficial.

    *   **Limited Experimental Scope:** The experiments are primarily focused on agent simulation. Demonstrating the benefits of SE(2) Fourier on other driving tasks (e.g., motion forecasting) or other areas of robotics (e.g., ground-based navigation) would strengthen the paper's impact.

    *   **Comparison to baselines**: The comparison of SE(2) Fourier with other SE(2) encoding schemes is limited.

*   **Potential Impact:**

    *   The linear memory scaling could enable training of more complex and scalable SE(2) invariant models for autonomous driving and robotics.
    *   The approach could inspire further research into efficient approximations for other group-invariant operations.

**Rationale for Score:**

The paper presents a genuinely novel and technically sound approach to SE(2) invariant attention. The ability to achieve linear memory scaling is a significant step forward for large-scale multi-agent systems. While the paper has some limitations in terms of experimental scope and further analysis of approximation error, the potential impact on the field warrants a high score.

### Demystify Protein Generation with Hierarchical Conditional Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18603v1)
- **Authors**: Zinan Ling, Yi Shi, Da Yan, Yang Zhou, Bo Hui
- **Abstract**: Generating novel and functional protein sequences is critical to a wide range of applications in biology. Recent advancements in conditional diffusion models have shown impressive empirical performance in protein generation tasks. However, reliable generations of protein remain an open research question in de novo protein design, especially when it comes to conditional diffusion models. Considering the biological function of a protein is determined by multi-level structures, we propose a novel multi-level conditional diffusion model that integrates both sequence-based and structure-based information for efficient end-to-end protein design guided by specified functions. By generating representations at different levels simultaneously, our framework can effectively model the inherent hierarchical relations between different levels, resulting in an informative and discriminative representation of the generated protein. We also propose a Protein-MMD, a new reliable evaluation metric, to evaluate the quality of generated protein with conditional diffusion models. Our new metric is able to capture both distributional and functional similarities between real and generated protein sequences while ensuring conditional consistency. We experiment with the benchmark datasets, and the results on conditional protein generation tasks demonstrate the efficacy of the proposed generation framework and evaluation metric.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of de novo protein design using conditional diffusion models. It proposes a novel multi-level conditional diffusion model that integrates both sequence-based and structure-based information at the amino acid, backbone, and all-atom levels. This hierarchical approach aims to better model the inherent relationships between different levels of protein structure, leading to more informative protein representations.  The authors also introduce a new evaluation metric, Protein-MMD, designed to assess conditional consistency in protein generation by capturing distributional and functional similarities.  Experiments on benchmark datasets demonstrate the efficacy of the proposed framework and evaluation metric.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the multi-level hierarchical diffusion model for protein generation and the Protein-MMD evaluation metric. While conditional diffusion models exist for protein generation, integrating information at multiple structural levels in this specific manner and proposing an improved evaluation metric is a non-trivial contribution. Current methods often focus on a single level of representation, neglecting crucial inter-level relationships. The SE(3) invariance also adds some novelty to facilitate more accurate protein structure generation.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a crucial challenge in protein design: ensuring that generated proteins are not only novel but also functional and consistent with given conditions. Traditional methods are often time-consuming and task-specific. An end-to-end conditional generative model offers a promising solution. The development of Protein-MMD addresses a critical gap in evaluating the quality and conditional consistency of generated proteins, which has been a major limitation in the field. The potential to generate diverse, novel, and functional proteins could have a significant impact on various applications in biotechnology and drug discovery. However, the scale of benefit remains uncertain based on the provided results.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Multi-Level Approach:** The hierarchical diffusion model is a conceptually sound approach for capturing the complex relationships between protein sequence, structure, and function.
    *   **Protein-MMD Metric:** The proposed evaluation metric directly addresses the limitations of existing metrics in assessing conditional consistency.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** Experiments on standard datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of the model and the evaluation metric. Ablation studies further highlight the importance of the multi-level approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Quantitative Improvement:** While the Protein-MMD outperforms baseline metrics, the margin of improvement is only modest in some cases.
    *   **Case Study Limitations:** The case study is helpful for illustration, but does not provide a decisive comparison in all experimental scenarios.
    *   **Real-world Validation:** The paper lacks a real-world validation of generated proteins. While the in-silico evaluations are promising, demonstrating the functionality of these proteins in vitro or in vivo would significantly strengthen the paper.
    *   **Baseline Performance Variation:** The performance of baselines can vary significantly, indicating challenges in their application across diverse datasets and potential sensitivity to hyperparameter tuning.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more sophisticated protein generation models. The hierarchical approach and the Protein-MMD evaluation metric could become standard components in future research. The limitations mentioned above, however, need to be addressed to unlock the full potential of this work.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of protein generation with some novelty and tackles a major problem. The multi-level diffusion approach and Protein-MMD metric are well-motivated and show promise. The experiments provide some empirical evidence to support the claims. However, the modest quantitative improvements, lack of real-world validation, and the limitations of case studies suggest that the work is not yet a breakthrough.

### Explainable Mapper: Charting LLM Embedding Spaces Using Perturbation-Based Explanation and Verification Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18607v1)
- **Authors**: Xinyuan Yan, Rita Sevastjanova, Sinie van der Ben, Mennatallah El-Assady, Bei Wang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) produce high-dimensional embeddings that capture rich semantic and syntactic relationships between words, sentences, and concepts. Investigating the topological structures of LLM embedding spaces via mapper graphs enables us to understand their underlying structures. Specifically, a mapper graph summarizes the topological structure of the embedding space, where each node represents a topological neighborhood (containing a cluster of embeddings), and an edge connects two nodes if their corresponding neighborhoods overlap. However, manually exploring these embedding spaces to uncover encoded linguistic properties requires considerable human effort. To address this challenge, we introduce a framework for semi-automatic annotation of these embedding properties. To organize the exploration process, we first define a taxonomy of explorable elements within a mapper graph such as nodes, edges, paths, components, and trajectories. The annotation of these elements is executed through two types of customizable LLM-based agents that employ perturbation techniques for scalable and automated analysis. These agents help to explore and explain the characteristics of mapper elements and verify the robustness of the generated explanations. We instantiate the framework within a visual analytics workspace and demonstrate its effectiveness through case studies. In particular, we replicate findings from prior research on BERT's embedding properties across various layers of its architecture and provide further observations into the linguistic properties of topological neighborhoods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Explainable Mapper," a framework for exploring and understanding the topological structure of embedding spaces generated by Large Language Models (LLMs). LLMs encode complex semantic and syntactic relationships, and the paper utilizes mapper graphs (a tool from topological data analysis) to visualize these embedding spaces, revealing clusters, connections, and transitions. To aid in the analysis, the framework introduces two types of LLM-based "mapper agents": Explanation Agents (to generate possible explanations for mapper elements) and Verification Agents (to verify the robustness of these explanations). The agents leverage operations like summarization, comparison, and perturbation. The paper demonstrates the framework's utility within a visual analytics workspace, including replication studies that reproduce prior research on BERT embedding properties, and offers additional observations on the linguistic characteristics of topological neighborhoods within the embedding space.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses an important problem:**  Understanding LLM embedding spaces is crucial for model developers, computational linguists, and NLP experts. The Explainable Mapper tackles the challenge of interpreting the complex structures encoded within these spaces.
    *   **Novel approach:** The combination of mapper graphs with LLM-based agents (Explanation and Verification) provides a novel way to semi-automate the annotation and analysis of embedding properties. The use of perturbation techniques for scalable and automated analysis is a key innovation.
    *   **Well-defined framework:** The paper carefully outlines the taxonomy of explorable elements in the mapper graph (nodes, edges, paths, etc.) and defines the roles and operations of the mapper agents.
    *   **Visual Analytics Workspace:** The instantiation of the framework within a visual analytics workspace makes it accessible and interactive for users.
    *   **Replication Studies:** Successfully replicating findings from prior research (e.g., BERT's layer-wise encoding of linguistic features) increases confidence in the framework's validity.
    *   **Contribution to Explainable AI:** Provides a valuable tool for making LLMs more transparent and interpretable, contributing to the broader field of Explainable AI (XAI).

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on LLMs:** The framework's performance is inherently tied to the capabilities and limitations of the underlying LLMs (e.g., GPT-4 for the agents). The potential for hallucinations or biases in LLM-generated explanations needs careful consideration. The paper acknowledges this limitation and proposes future mitigation strategies but doesn't fully address the problem within the current implementation.
    *   **Perturbation Strategy Limitations:** The reliance on single-token perturbations might not be sufficient to capture all the nuances of embedding behavior. Some explanations may prove insufficiently robust if a more significant perturbation is utilized.
    *   **User-dependent trajectory verification:** The fact that user currently is doing the trajectory verification makes the system less scalable and automatic.
    *   **Scalability Concern:** While the agents automate some analysis, manual interaction is still required for navigation, selection, and interpretation. Scaling the framework to extremely large embedding spaces and complex linguistic phenomena might pose challenges.
    *   **Limited evaluation scope:** The case studies primarily focus on replicating existing knowledge about BERT. More exploration of novel discoveries or insights enabled by the Explainable Mapper would further demonstrate its potential.

*   **Novelty and Significance:** The paper is significant as it provides a practical tool that makes the abstract nature of LLM embeddings tangible and explorable, using proven visualization techniques combined with the power of the LLMs themselves. The idea of using LLMs to assist in understanding LLM representations is interesting. The approach is innovative but not without its reliance on those models, which the authors acknowledge. However, a substantial impact on the community of researchers is needed to raise the value of the article.

*   **Potential Influence:** The Explainable Mapper could influence how researchers and practitioners investigate LLM behavior, develop more robust models, and analyze linguistic patterns. The agent-based framework could inspire similar approaches in other domains where high-dimensional data needs to be interpreted.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a valuable and innovative framework for a difficult and important problem. Its strengths lie in its clear conceptualization, well-defined agents, and demonstration of utility through replication studies. However, the reliance on LLMs, limitations in evaluation scope, and scalability concerns prevent it from reaching a higher score. The paper offers a significant step forward in the field of LLM interpretability and has the potential to inspire future research, but needs additional refinement to fully realize its potential.
- **Classification**: cs.CG
- **Score**: 7/10

### TRPrompt: Bootstrapping Query-Aware Prompt Optimization from Textual Rewards
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18618v1)
- **Authors**: Andreea Nica, Ivan Zakazov, Nicolas Mario Baldwin, Saibo Geng, Robert West
- **Abstract**: Prompt optimization improves the reasoning abilities of large language models (LLMs) without requiring parameter updates to the target model. Following heuristic-based "Think step by step" approaches, the field has evolved in two main directions: while one group of methods uses textual feedback to elicit improved prompts from general-purpose LLMs in a training-free way, a concurrent line of research relies on numerical rewards to train a special prompt model, tailored for providing optimal prompts to the target model. In this paper, we introduce the Textual Reward Prompt framework (TRPrompt), which unifies these approaches by directly incorporating textual feedback into training of the prompt model. Our framework does not require prior dataset collection and is being iteratively improved with the feedback on the generated prompts. When coupled with the capacity of an LLM to internalize the notion of what a "good" prompt is, the high-resolution signal provided by the textual rewards allows us to train a prompt model yielding state-of-the-art query-specific prompts for the problems from the challenging math datasets GSMHard and MATH.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the TRPrompt paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TRPrompt, a novel framework for optimizing prompts for Large Language Models (LLMs) *without* updating the LLM's parameters.  TRPrompt leverages *textual* rewards to guide the training of a separate prompt model. This contrasts with existing approaches that rely on either numerical rewards or training-free iterative refinement using off-the-shelf LLMs.  TRPrompt iteratively improves a prompt model by creating a synthetic dataset of prompts and their corresponding textual rewards (generated by another LLM), fine-tuning the prompt model on this dataset, and then updating the textual reward through an approach that approximates gradients in the discrete text space. The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on challenging mathematical reasoning tasks (GSMHard and MATH).  A key contribution is the use of textual rewards to provide richer supervisory signals for training the prompt model.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The idea of using textual rewards isn't entirely new, as they've been used in related fields like reward model training.  However, TRPrompt's innovation lies in directly incorporating these textual rewards *into* the training loop of a prompt model. This contrasts sharply with previous approaches.  The idea of bootstrapping a prompt model through iterative self-improvement with a feedback loop (textual rewards) is also a significant contribution. While iterative self-improvement has been explored, the way it's done here within the prompting context and the use of textual feedback makes it relatively unique. The overall architecture and training methodology represent a novel combination of existing techniques.

*   **Significance:**  The significance of the paper is twofold:
    *   *Improved Performance:*  The empirical results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on challenging math reasoning datasets (GSMHard and MATH).  This suggests that textual rewards can indeed provide a more effective supervisory signal than numerical rewards in this context.
    *   *Broader Applicability:* The framework opens avenues for prompt optimization in tasks where defining numerical rewards is difficult or impossible.  Creative writing, poetry generation, and other open-ended tasks could potentially benefit.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   *Strong Empirical Results:*  The paper presents compelling evidence of TRPrompt's effectiveness on complex math reasoning tasks.
    *   *Clear Methodology:*  The methodology is well-described, and the iterative training process is clearly explained.
    *   *Ablation Studies:* The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of different components of the framework. They highlight the importance of both Supervised Fine-Tuning and Optimal Reward Search (TextGrad).
    *   *Cross-Dataset Evaluation:* The cross-dataset evaluation provides evidence of TRPrompt's generalization capability, even though performance drops when applied to out-of-distribution datasets.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   *Computational Cost:* The paper acknowledges the high computational cost of the Optimal Reward Search (TextGrad) step, which involves a computationally expensive iterative process. This limits the scalability of the approach.
    *   *Dependence on the Reward Model:** The quality of the textual rewards is crucial for the success of TRPrompt. If the LLM used as the reward model generates poor or inconsistent feedback, it can negatively impact the training process. While the authors use the same model family (Llama-3-8B-Instruct), a more robust investigation into the impact of reward model quality would be beneficial.
    *   *Limited Improvement on Easier Datasets:* The paper notes that TRPrompt shows less improvement on the GSM8K dataset, potentially because the target model already performs well on this task. This suggests that textual rewards may be less effective when the target model's performance is already high.

*   **Potential Influence:**  TRPrompt has the potential to influence research in prompt engineering, especially in tasks where numerical rewards are difficult to define. The idea of using textual rewards and iterative self-improvement could inspire new approaches to prompt optimization and LLM alignment. The shift towards more informative signals for training is definitely going to influence how the prompt optimization is performed going forward.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7**.  While TRPrompt is not a "groundbreaking" paradigm shift, it represents a significant and novel contribution to the field of prompt engineering. The core idea of using textual rewards within a training loop is very promising. The state-of-the-art results on challenging benchmarks demonstrate the practical effectiveness of the approach. The weaknesses related to computational cost and potential dependence on reward model quality are legitimate concerns. These are areas that need to be addressed in future research. However, overall, the paper offers compelling evidence that textual rewards can be a powerful tool for prompt optimization and, therefore, deserves a rating of 7.

### Captain Cinema: Towards Short Movie Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18634v1)
- **Authors**: Junfei Xiao, Ceyuan Yang, Lvmin Zhang, Shengqu Cai, Yang Zhao, Yuwei Guo, Gordon Wetzstein, Maneesh Agrawala, Alan Yuille, Lu Jiang
- **Abstract**: We present Captain Cinema, a generation framework for short movie generation. Given a detailed textual description of a movie storyline, our approach firstly generates a sequence of keyframes that outline the entire narrative, which ensures long-range coherence in both the storyline and visual appearance (e.g., scenes and characters). We refer to this step as top-down keyframe planning. These keyframes then serve as conditioning signals for a video synthesis model, which supports long context learning, to produce the spatio-temporal dynamics between them. This step is referred to as bottom-up video synthesis. To support stable and efficient generation of multi-scene long narrative cinematic works, we introduce an interleaved training strategy for Multimodal Diffusion Transformers (MM-DiT), specifically adapted for long-context video data. Our model is trained on a specially curated cinematic dataset consisting of interleaved data pairs. Our experiments demonstrate that Captain Cinema performs favorably in the automated creation of visually coherent and narrative consistent short movies in high quality and efficiency. Project page: https://thecinema.ai
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Captain Cinema: Towards Short Movie Generation" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Captain Cinema, a novel framework for short movie generation from textual descriptions.  The system consists of two main modules: a top-down keyframe planner that generates a sequence of keyframes outlining the narrative and a bottom-up video synthesizer that produces the video dynamics between these keyframes. A key component is "GoldenMem," a memory mechanism designed to maintain character and scene consistency over longer durations by selectively compressing and retaining contextual information from past keyframes. The method employs an interleaved training strategy for Multimodal Diffusion Transformers (MM-DiT) and is trained on a curated cinematic dataset.  Experiments demonstrate the framework's ability to generate visually coherent and narratively consistent short movies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates innovation by integrating both top-down keyframe planning and bottom-up video synthesis for longer narrative generation, which is more significant than just synthesizing short clips. The *GoldenMem* approach to compressing long-context visual memory is also a novel contribution. The interleaved training strategy for MM-DiT for long-context data is also unique.
*   **Significance:** Generating longer, narrative-driven videos with visual and narrative coherence is a significant challenge in the field of video generation. This work tackles this challenge head-on. The results, while qualitative, are compelling and demonstrate a clear advancement over existing methods. The framework could open new avenues for automated content creation and storytelling.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The two-stage approach effectively decouples high-level narrative planning from low-level video synthesis, leading to improved coherence and quality.
    *   The *GoldenMem* mechanism provides a practical solution for managing long-range dependencies in video generation, a critical factor for narrative consistency.
    *   The results are promising, demonstrating the generation of multi-scene videos with relatively consistent characters and environments.
    * The detail of the ablations and their justification are thorough.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The evaluation relies heavily on qualitative results and automatic metrics from VBench. A comprehensive user study that quantitatively assesses the narrative coherence and engagement of the generated movies would strengthen the paper.
    *   While the paper mentions addressing limitations such as dataset scarcity, there is still an implied reliance on large language models (Gemini) for generating storylines, which could be a bottleneck.
    *   Generalization abilities, as explored in section 4.3, while interesting, do not clearly delineate when they might fall short (beyond characters and storylines dissimilar from the training data), making it difficult to assess the generalizability of the approach.
    * There is not sufficient evaluation that explores when the models break- e.g., what prompt complexities might be too much?

*   **Impact:**
    *   The paper presents a strong foundation for future research on long-form video generation. It could inspire new techniques for memory management, hierarchical video synthesis, and controllable narrative generation.
    *   The generated videos exhibit better narrative coherence and quality than previous approaches, potentially enabling more sophisticated applications in automated content creation.
    * The work may be a useful point to compare future video generation efforts.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel framework for generating longer videos with improved narrative and visual coherence. The integration of top-down planning and bottom-up synthesis, along with the *GoldenMem* approach, represents a significant step forward in the field. However, the evaluation lacks thorough quantitative user studies, and there are limitations in generalizability and prompt complexity management. The method is therefore a solid contribution but could be more rigorous in how it is assessed. It demonstrates a clear advancement, warranting a rating above average, but falls short of being a truly exceptional breakthrough in the field. The relatively complex training and inference strategies may also limit its accessibility to other researchers.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### CatchPhrase: EXPrompt-Guided Encoder Adaptation for Audio-to-Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18750v1)
- **Authors**: Hyunwoo Oh, SeungJu Cha, Kwanyoung Lee, Si-Woo Kim, Dong-Jin Kim
- **Abstract**: We propose CatchPhrase, a novel audio-to-image generation framework designed to mitigate semantic misalignment between audio inputs and generated images. While recent advances in multi-modal encoders have enabled progress in cross-modal generation, ambiguity stemming from homographs and auditory illusions continues to hinder accurate alignment. To address this issue, CatchPhrase generates enriched cross-modal semantic prompts (EXPrompt Mining) from weak class labels by leveraging large language models (LLMs) and audio captioning models (ACMs). To address both class-level and instance-level misalignment, we apply multi-modal filtering and retrieval to select the most semantically aligned prompt for each audio sample (EXPrompt Selector). A lightweight mapping network is then trained to adapt pre-trained text-to-image generation models to audio input. Extensive experiments on multiple audio classification datasets demonstrate that CatchPhrase improves audio-to-image alignment and consistently enhances generation quality by mitigating semantic misalignment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CatchPhrase: EXPrompt-Guided Encoder Adaptation for Audio-to-Image Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CatchPhrase, a novel framework designed to improve the semantic alignment between audio inputs and generated images in audio-to-image generation tasks. The key idea is to address the semantic misalignment caused by limited information in weak audio class labels and phenomena like homographs and auditory illusions. CatchPhrase utilizes large language models (LLMs) and audio captioning models (ACMs) to generate enriched cross-modal prompts (EXPrompts) from weak class labels. A multi-modal filtering and retrieval strategy (EXPrompt Selector) is then used to select the most semantically aligned prompt for each audio sample. Finally, a lightweight mapping network adapts a pre-trained text-to-image generation model to the audio input, guided by the selected prompts. Experimental results on several audio classification datasets demonstrate that CatchPhrase improves audio-to-image alignment and enhances generation quality by mitigating semantic misalignment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper offers a reasonably novel approach to audio-to-image generation by directly addressing the issue of semantic misalignment. Prior works like GlueGen use fixed templates that suffer from homographs and auditory illusions. The idea of generating EXPrompts by combining textual and auditory cues from LLMs and ACMs is a good one. The multi-modal filtering and retrieval mechanism (EXPrompt Selector) further refines the prompt selection process. The combination of these components to tackle the stated problem is the most novel aspect.

**Significance:** The problem of semantic misalignment in audio-to-image generation is important and often overlooked. A system producing misaligned outputs undermines user trust and limits practical applications. The proposed solution, if robust and generalizable, could have a positive impact on the field.  The empirical results do suggest that CatchPhrase improves the quality and alignment of generated images compared to existing methods on standard datasets. The user study further validates the improved perceptual alignment.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and explains the problem of semantic misalignment in audio-to-image generation.
*   **Well-Defined Approach:** The proposed CatchPhrase framework is well-structured and the components are logically connected.
*   **Use of LLMs and ACMs:** Leveraging LLMs and ACMs to generate enriched prompts is a creative way to address the limited expressiveness of class labels.
*   **Multi-Modal Filtering and Retrieval:** The EXPrompt Selector mechanism is important for fine-grained alignment.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides quantitative and qualitative results on standard datasets, showing the effectiveness of the proposed approach.  Ablation studies also isolate the impact of different components.
*   **User Study:** The inclusion of a user study enhances the validity of the results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependency on Pre-trained Models:** The framework relies heavily on the performance of pre-trained LLMs, ACMs, and text-to-image generation models. The overall performance is thus capped by the performance of these components.  Also, the quality of the generated prompts directly influences the performance of generated images, so performance could potentially suffer with poor prompts.
*   **Limited Generalizability:** The framework is evaluated on audio classification datasets. The performance on more complex and realistic audio scenes with overlapping events might be limited. The discussion in the limitation section confirms this.
*   **Incremental Improvement:** While CatchPhrase demonstrates improvement over existing methods, the gains might be considered incremental, particularly in quantitative metrics. The AIS improvements, while consistent, aren't extraordinarily large, suggesting there is room for much more improvement in this problem space. The paper has the most impact in improving the images that are generated using homographs.
*   **Computational Cost:** The use of LLMs and ACMs for prompt generation adds computational overhead. The paper does not provide a thorough analysis of the computational cost associated with the framework.
*   **Limited Comparison to Modern Methods:** Given the rapid advancement in text-to-image generation and diffusion modeling, some of the baseline models might already be somewhat outdated, and comparing to the very state-of-the-art would be necessary to prove the robustness of the idea.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of audio-to-image generation by specifically addressing the problem of semantic misalignment. While the gains might be incremental and the approach relies on pre-trained models, the proposed CatchPhrase framework is well-designed, empirically validated, and offers a novel solution to a relevant problem. The limitations related to generalizability and computational cost should be addressed in future work.

### Initial Steps in Integrating Large Reasoning and Action Models for Service Composition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18775v1)
- **Authors**: Ilche Georgievski, Marco Aiello
- **Abstract**: Service composition remains a central challenge in building adaptive and intelligent software systems, often constrained by limited reasoning capabilities or brittle execution mechanisms. This paper explores the integration of two emerging paradigms enabled by large language models: Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) and Large Action Models (LAMs). We argue that LRMs address the challenges of semantic reasoning and ecosystem complexity while LAMs excel in dynamic action execution and system interoperability. However, each paradigm has complementary limitations - LRMs lack grounded action capabilities, and LAMs often struggle with deep reasoning. We propose an integrated LRM-LAM architectural framework as a promising direction for advancing automated service composition. Such a system can reason about service requirements and constraints while dynamically executing workflows, thus bridging the gap between intention and execution. This integration has the potential to transform service composition into a fully automated, user-friendly process driven by high-level natural language intent.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, including a justified novelty/significance score.

**Summary:**
